People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope it's okay that I made an appointment for this. It's probably nothing too major, maybe just stress from my bookkeeping job, but... well, my sleep has just gotten really messed up lately.

I hope that makes sense? I just wasn't sure what else to do, and I really want to be doing the right thing to get back on track.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, I wouldn't necessarily say overwhelmed... I mean, I really don't want to sound like I can't handle things, because I usually do a good job of keeping it all together! It's probably just normal stress. But it is a little frustrating, I guess. 

My thoughts just start jumping around so fast. Like, I'll be lying in bed, and suddenly I'm thinking about mortgage payments, and then it switches right to my CPA study schedules, and then out of nowhere I'm fixated on the kitchen cabinets. It just goes so fast that I can't seem to slow my brain down enough to actually relax. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

Um, I guess sometimes? I really try to stay focused at work, because I want to do a good job, you know? Um, well, I don't think I talk *excessively*... I mean, I try really hard to be a good listener, you know? I don't want to be one of those people who just dominates the conversation. But... I guess there are times when I do talk a little more than usual. I hope that's alright to say. 

Like, the other day, I was talking to my sister, and I guess I was just really excited about a few different things? I was trying to explain some stuff from work, like equity and taxes, because I find it interesting, and then I kind of jumped to talking about new paint colors for my apartment... and she actually told me I was talking so fast she couldn't even get a word in.

Like I can't stop until I've explained everything perfectly. I hope that doesn't make me sound super annoying or anything. Is it normal to get a little carried away when you have a lot on your mind?

Um, no, I haven't taken any days off. I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I want them to know they can count on me. So I've been making sure I get to work every day.

Is it normal to get a little foggy like that when you're stressed? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or feeling like you have to keep talking?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I don't think I talk *excessively*... I mean, I try really hard to be a good listener, you know? I don't want to be one of those people who just dominates the conversation. But... I guess there are times when I do talk a little more than usual. I hope that's alright to say. \n\nLike, the other day, I was talking to my sister, and I guess I was just really excited about a few different things? I was trying to explain some stuff from work, like equity and taxes, because I find it interesting, and then I kind of jumped to talking about new paint colors for my apartment... and she actually told me I was talking so fast she couldn't even get a word in. \n\nI felt so bad! I didn't mean to talk over her. It's just... in those moments, I kind of feel this push inside me, like I just have to get all the words out right then and there. Like I can't stop until I've explained everything perfectly. I hope that doesn't make me sound super annoying or anything. Is it normal to get a little carried away when you have a lot on your mind?\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take any days off work due to how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't taken any days off. I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I want them to know they can count on me. So I've been making sure I get to work every day. \n\nBut... well, I guess I haven't been doing my best work lately. I hope it's just a phase, but I've actually made a few mistakes with the reconciliations. Just little things, but enough of them that my supervisor actually pulled me aside to talk about it. I felt terrible, honestly. I promised her I'd do better. \n\nAnd outside of work... I don't know, I guess I haven't been doing much. I usually go to this salsa class, which I love, but I've been skipping it. And I haven't really been seeing my friends, either. I just... I don't quite feel like myself right now, so I've been avoiding everyone. I hope that doesn't sound too awful. I just don't want to bring anyone else down when I'm feeling off, you know? Is it okay that I'm just trying to lay low for a bit?\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed any changes in your relationships with family or friends due to how you've been feeling lately?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 26 / bookkeeping_accounting_or_auditing_clerk / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Earlier in the month I felt almost lit up and unstoppable, then I’d snap at my mom or my supervisor over little questions that normally wouldn’t bother me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. Um, well... I really try to be a good daughter and a good friend. I don't like conflict at all, so I usually just try to keep the peace. But... I guess things have been a little bit tense lately. I hope you don't think I'm a bad person for saying this.

It's just, earlier this month, I was actually feeling really, really good. Like, almost lit up from the inside? I felt like I could take on the world and get everything done, which was great for a while. But then... I don't know, I started getting really easily frustrated.

I felt so guilty afterward! I always apologize right away because I hate making anyone upset. I just... I don't know why I get so snappy when I'm feeling that energized. Um, well... I guess I kind of just mentioned that, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right. 

But yes, um, that's exactly what happens. Like I was saying, earlier this month, I had this stretch where I felt just... amazing. Like I was lit up from the inside and completely unstoppable. I was getting so much done, and I felt so capable. I really liked feeling that way, to be honest. 

But then... yeah, the irritability kind of sneaks in. Like I mentioned with my mom and my supervisor... they would just ask me these tiny, normal questions, and I would just snap at them. It's almost like my brain is moving so fast that any little interruption just feels incredibly frustrating? I don't mean to be short with them, I really don't. I try so hard to be polite and respectful to everyone. It's just... in those moments, it's like I can't help it. 

I always feel terrible about it later. I don't want to be a difficult person to be around. Does that make sense? Like, is it okay that I get a little overwhelmed when I have all that energy? I'm really trying my best to manage it so I don't hurt anyone's feelings.

I just don't have one specific worry that's keeping me up, it's just a lot of little things all going at the same time. Um, let me think... I want to make sure I give you the right timeline so I'm being helpful. The really obvious cycling... like, going between that super charged-up stretch and then the... the crash, I guess? It's been going on for about a month now.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I'm not entirely sure exactly how many days they last? I'm sorry, I should probably be keeping better track of these things so I can give you a better answer. I really want to be accurate for you. 

I guess... they just sort of happen for a few days at a time? Like, I'll have that really charged-up feeling for a bit, and then it just kind of fades into the irritable part, or I end up crashing. I don't have an exact number right now. Does that make sense?

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF31.9, F90.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope it's okay that I made an appointment for this. It's probably nothing too major, maybe just stress from my bookkeeping job, but... well, my sleep has just gotten really messed up lately. \n\nI try not to make a big deal out of it, but I'll either end up staying awake for half the night because my mind is just running, or I'll wake up way too early and feel like I never really rested at all. I hope that makes sense? I just wasn't sure what else to do, and I really want to be doing the right thing to get back on track.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed when your mind is racing at night?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't necessarily say overwhelmed... I mean, I really don't want to sound like I can't handle things, because I usually do a good job of keeping it all together! It's probably just normal stress. But it is a little frustrating, I guess. \n\nMy thoughts just start jumping around so fast. Like, I'll be lying in bed, and suddenly I'm thinking about mortgage payments, and then it switches right to my CPA study schedules, and then out of nowhere I'm fixated on the kitchen cabinets. It just goes so fast that I can't seem to slow my brain down enough to actually relax. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that's been on your mind or worrying you when you lie in bed at night?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really anything specific. I mean, I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just worrying about nothing, because I know I have a lot to be thankful for. It's just... it's not like there's one big bad thing I'm stressing over. \n\nIt's more like my brain just wants to process every little detail of the day, or, you know, plan out the entire week all at once. Like I'm just trying to be really prepared for everything? I hope that's a normal thing to do. I just don't have one specific worry that's keeping me up, it's just a lot of little things all going at the same time. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling restless or fidgety during the day?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess sometimes? I really try to stay focused at work, because I want to do a good job, you know? But... well, I have noticed that sometimes I'm just sitting there, staring at the calculator for a minute before I even press the buttons. It's like I'm just moving a little slower than usual. I hope that doesn't make me sound lazy or anything! \n\nBut then other times, it's almost the opposite? Like I'll be fidgeting with the receipts on my desk and I just can't seem to sit completely still. I try to hide it so my boss doesn't think I'm distracted. I'm sure everyone gets a little restless sometimes, right? I just hope I'm giving you the right kind of examples.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble concentrating on tasks during the day due to your mind racing?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really try my best to stay on top of everything at work, because I don't want anyone to think I'm slacking off. But, yeah, I guess I have been having a little bit of trouble focusing lately. I hope that's okay to admit. \n\nIt's just that these spreadsheets I used to handle so easily, they kind of... blur together now? Like, I'll catch myself rereading the exact same row of numbers over and over again. And even after I do that, I still don't completely trust what I just entered. I always double-check my work anyway, because I want to be thorough, but it's taking me a lot longer than it usually does. I really hope my boss hasn't noticed. Is it normal to get a little foggy like that when you're stressed? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or feeling like you have to keep talking?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I don't think I talk *excessively*... I mean, I try really hard to be a good listener, you know? I don't want to be one of those people who just dominates the conversation. But... I guess there are times when I do talk a little more than usual. I hope that's alright to say. \n\nLike, the other day, I was talking to my sister, and I guess I was just really excited about a few different things? I was trying to explain some stuff from work, like equity and taxes, because I find it interesting, and then I kind of jumped to talking about new paint colors for my apartment... and she actually told me I was talking so fast she couldn't even get a word in. \n\nI felt so bad! I didn't mean to talk over her. It's just... in those moments, I kind of feel this push inside me, like I just have to get all the words out right then and there. Like I can't stop until I've explained everything perfectly. I hope that doesn't make me sound super annoying or anything. Is it normal to get a little carried away when you have a lot on your mind?\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take any days off work due to how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't taken any days off. I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I want them to know they can count on me. So I've been making sure I get to work every day. \n\nBut... well, I guess I haven't been doing my best work lately. I hope it's just a phase, but I've actually made a few mistakes with the reconciliations. Just little things, but enough of them that my supervisor actually pulled me aside to talk about it. I felt terrible, honestly. I promised her I'd do better. \n\nAnd outside of work... I don't know, I guess I haven't been doing much. I usually go to this salsa class, which I love, but I've been skipping it. And I haven't really been seeing my friends, either. I just... I don't quite feel like myself right now, so I've been avoiding everyone. I hope that doesn't sound too awful. I just don't want to bring anyone else down when I'm feeling off, you know? Is it okay that I'm just trying to lay low for a bit?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with family or friends due to how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really try to be a good daughter and a good friend. I don't like conflict at all, so I usually just try to keep the peace. But... I guess things have been a little bit tense lately. I hope you don't think I'm a bad person for saying this.\n\nIt's just, earlier this month, I was actually feeling really, really good. Like, almost lit up from the inside? I felt like I could take on the world and get everything done, which was great for a while. But then... I don't know, I started getting really easily frustrated. \n\nLike, my mom would ask me a simple question, or my supervisor would just check in on something small, and I would just... snap at them. Over things that normally wouldn't bother me at all. I felt so guilty afterward! I always apologize right away because I hate making anyone upset. I just... I don't know why I get so snappy when I'm feeling that energized. Is it normal to get a little irritable when you're feeling really hyped up like that? I'm really trying to work on it, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have periods where you feel extremely energetic and then suddenly feel frustrated or irritable?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I kind of just mentioned that, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right. \n\nBut yes, um, that's exactly what happens. Like I was saying, earlier this month, I had this stretch where I felt just... amazing. Like I was lit up from the inside and completely unstoppable. I was getting so much done, and I felt so capable. I really liked feeling that way, to be honest. \n\nBut then... yeah, the irritability kind of sneaks in. Like I mentioned with my mom and my supervisor... they would just ask me these tiny, normal questions, and I would just snap at them. It's almost like my brain is moving so fast that any little interruption just feels incredibly frustrating? I don't mean to be short with them, I really don't. I try so hard to be polite and respectful to everyone. It's just... in those moments, it's like I can't help it. \n\nI always feel terrible about it later. I don't want to be a difficult person to be around. Does that make sense? Like, is it okay that I get a little overwhelmed when I have all that energy? I'm really trying my best to manage it so I don't hurt anyone's feelings.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have these periods of feeling extremely energetic followed by irritability?\n\nPatient: Um, yes, I think we... we just talked about that a little bit, right? I hope it's okay that I already brought it up, I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information. \n\nBut yes, it does happen. Like I was saying, earlier in the month I had a few days where I felt almost... lit up? Just totally unstoppable and on top of everything. It felt really good at first. But then, yeah, I'd end up snapping at my mom or my supervisor over these really little questions. Things that normally wouldn't bother me at all, you know? \n\nI feel so bad when I do it because I really, really don't want to be a difficult person. I always try to be easy to work with and a good daughter. I just get so... keyed up, I guess? I hope you don't think I'm just a mean person. I really try to catch myself and apologize. Is it normal for that kind of energy to turn into frustration like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you have periods where you feel extremely happy or elevated, separate from the times when you feel energetic?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm so sorry, I feel like I'm doing a bad job of explaining this since we keep coming back to it! I really want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. \n\nUm... for me, they kind of feel like the same thing? Like, when I was saying that earlier in the month I felt almost lit up and unstoppable... that was me feeling really happy and energetic all at once. It's a really great feeling at first. But... it's during that exact same time that I'd end up snapping at my mom or my supervisor over those little questions. It's not really a separate period of time. The happy, elevated feeling just sort of... turns into me being irritable about things that normally wouldn't bother me at all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm answering it right this time... I just really don't want to confuse you, and I promise I'm usually a very patient person at work and at home.\n\nDoctor: How often do you experience these periods of feeling extremely happy or elevated followed by irritability?\n\nPatient: Um, let me think... I want to make sure I give you the right timeline so I'm being helpful. The really obvious cycling... like, going between that super charged-up stretch and then the... the crash, I guess? It's been going on for about a month now. \n\nIt really started right around the time my mortgage became real. I mean, taking on a mortgage is a huge deal, right? So I just kept telling myself it was normal stress and I was just handling it a little clumsily... I usually try to manage my stress much better than that, I promise. But yeah, it's been about a month of this really noticeable back-and-forth. Is that the kind of detail you were looking for? I hope that helps.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or elevated and also experienced a decreased need for sleep?\n\nPatient: Um, yes... I actually did notice that. I hope it doesn't make me sound like I'm not taking care of myself, because I usually try really hard to get a full eight hours so I'm sharp for my bookkeeping job. I really do try to be responsible with my schedule. \n\nBut during those days we were just talking about... there were several nights where I only slept maybe three or four hours? And the weird thing is, I didn't feel tired at all. I still got up feeling totally charged. Like, coffee wasn't even necessary, I was just ready to go. \n\nIs... is that what you mean? I hope I'm giving you the right examples. I just want to make sure I'm being as helpful as possible.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling extremely happy or elevated usually last?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'm not entirely sure exactly how many days they last? I'm sorry, I should probably be keeping better track of these things so I can give you a better answer. I really want to be accurate for you. \n\nI guess... they just sort of happen for a few days at a time? Like, I'll have that really charged-up feeling for a bit, and then it just kind of fades into the irritable part, or I end up crashing. I haven't really counted the exact number of days. I hope that's okay? I can definitely start keeping a journal or something if that would make this easier for you to figure out! I just... I don't have an exact number right now. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean... I'm an accountant, well, a bookkeeping clerk, so being careful and thinking things through is basically my whole job. I'm usually very, very responsible. I always try to weigh the pros and cons before I do anything, I promise. \n\nI guess... maybe sometimes when I'm feeling really, really good, I might buy something small that I didn't strictly plan for? But nothing crazy, just like... an extra coffee or a nice candle. I definitely don't do anything reckless or dangerous. I always try to make good choices. Is that the kind of thing you mean? I hope I'm not coming across as irresponsible... I really do try my best to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of feeling extremely low or depressed after one of these energetic and elevated periods?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. Yes, I have. I hope you don't think less of me for it, because I really try to stay positive and be a good employee and a good person, but... lately, after those energized days, I just... I crash. \n\nIt's been really hard recently. I'll wake up and just have this... this incredibly heavy, empty feeling? It's so silly, because I have a nice life. I have my own condo that I worked so, so hard to get, and I should be so grateful and happy about it. But sometimes I just sit in my living room and... I just want to cry. For no good reason. \n\nI really try to push through it, I promise. I don't want to be a burden or seem ungrateful for what I have. But it just feels so overwhelmingly sad sometimes. Is... is it normal to feel like that after having a lot of energy? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Um... well, I guess... maybe a little bit? I hope it doesn't make me sound terrible, because I really pride myself on being professional and kind to everyone. I try so hard to be easy to work with.

Like, I'll catch myself rereading the exact same row of numbers over and over again. And even after I do that, I still don't completely trust what I just entered. I always double-check my work anyway, because I want to be thorough, but it's taking me a lot longer than it usually does. I really hope my boss hasn't noticed. Is it normal to get a little foggy like that when you're stressed? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or feeling like you have to keep talking?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I don't think I talk *excessively*... I mean, I try really hard to be a good listener, you know? I don't want to be one of those people who just dominates the conversation. But... I guess there are times when I do talk a little more than usual. I hope that's alright to say. \n\nLike, the other day, I was talking to my sister, and I guess I was just really excited about a few different things? I was trying to explain some stuff from work, like equity and taxes, because I find it interesting, and then I kind of jumped to talking about new paint colors for my apartment... and she actually told me I was talking so fast she couldn't even get a word in. \n\nI felt so bad! I didn't mean to talk over her. It's just... in those moments, I kind of feel this push inside me, like I just have to get all the words out right then and there. Like I can't stop until I've explained everything perfectly. I hope that doesn't make me sound super annoying or anything. Is it normal to get a little carried away when you have a lot on your mind?\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take any days off work due to how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't taken any days off. I really pride myself on being reliable, you know? I want them to know they can count on me. So I've been making sure I get to work every day. \n\nBut... well, I guess I haven't been doing my best work lately. I hope it's just a phase, but I've actually made a few mistakes with the reconciliations. Just little things, but enough of them that my supervisor actually pulled me aside to talk about it. I felt terrible, honestly. I promised her I'd do better. \n\nAnd outside of work... I don't know, I guess I haven't been doing much. I usually go to this salsa class, which I love, but I've been skipping it. And I haven't really been seeing my friends, either. I just... I don't quite feel like myself right now, so I've been avoiding everyone. I hope that doesn't sound too awful. I just don't want to bring anyone else down when I'm feeling off, you know? Is it okay that I'm just trying to lay low for a bit?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with family or friends due to how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really try to be a good daughter and a good friend. I don't like conflict at all, so I usually just try to keep the peace. But... I guess things have been a little bit tense lately. I hope you don't think I'm a bad person for saying this.\n\nIt's just, earlier this month, I was actually feeling really, really good. Like, almost lit up from the inside? I felt like I could take on the world and get everything done, which was great for a while. But then... I don't know, I started getting really easily frustrated. \n\nLike, my mom would ask me a simple question, or my supervisor would just check in on something small, and I would just... snap at them. Over things that normally wouldn't bother me at all. I felt so guilty afterward! I always apologize right away because I hate making anyone upset. I just... I don't know why I get so snappy when I'm feeling that energized. Is it normal to get a little irritable when you're feeling really hyped up like that? I'm really trying to work on it, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have periods where you feel extremely energetic and then suddenly feel frustrated or irritable?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I kind of just mentioned that, didn't I? I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right. \n\nBut yes, um, that's exactly what happens. Like I was saying, earlier this month, I had this stretch where I felt just... amazing. Like I was lit up from the inside and completely unstoppable. I was getting so much done, and I felt so capable. I really liked feeling that way, to be honest. \n\nBut then... yeah, the irritability kind of sneaks in. It's almost like my brain is moving so fast that any little interruption just feels incredibly frustrating? I don't mean to be short with them, I really don't. I try so hard to be polite and respectful to everyone. It's just... in those moments, it's like I can't help it. \n\nI always feel terrible about it later. I don't want to be a difficult person to be around. Does that make sense? Like, is it okay that I get a little overwhelmed when I have all that energy? I'm really trying my best to manage it so I don't hurt anyone's feelings.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have these periods of feeling extremely energetic followed by irritability?\n\nPatient: Um, yes, I think we... we just talked about that a little bit, right? I hope it's okay that I already brought it up, I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information. \n\nBut yes, it does happen. Like I was saying, earlier in the month I had a few days where I felt almost... lit up? Just totally unstoppable and on top of everything. It felt really good at first.
